nginx security releases of November 2026: am I affected and what to do

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

nginx published security releases on November 18, 2026 (stable 1.30.7, mainline 1.31.9), followed by a smaller one on November 23 (1.30.8 and 1.31.10). If nginx serves your website or sits in front of your application, here is how to check whether you are concerned and what to do.

According to the official nginx security advisories page, the November 18 release fixes several issues, including memory corruption in HTTP/2 flow control (CVE-2026-123007), use-after-free bugs in HTTP/2, HTTP/3, the resolver and the mail proxy module, an infinite loop with TLS 1.3 request bodies, and a buffer overflow with alias and try_files (CVE-2026-125283). The November 23 release fixes excessive CPU usage with ssl_early_data (CVE-2026-132779).

Not every issue applies to every setup. For example, the HTTP/3 issue only matters if you enabled HTTP/3, the mail proxy issue only if you use the mail module, and the ssl_early_data issue only if that directive is on. Your configuration decides what is reachable.

I did not find a primary source confirming attacks in the wild. Check again before assuming either way.

If you are on managed hosting, a CDN or a platform that runs nginx for you, the provider normally patches it. Check their status or security notices.

An outside check cannot reliably read your patch level: many servers hide the nginx version, and that is a good thing. What you can verify for free, without an account, is the surrounding hygiene. The AttackScout public audit reads only public information and covers certificate, email authentication, security headers and more, with an A to F grade. If you want the headers on their own, use the security headers check.

PHP security release of November 19, 2026: am I affected and what to do

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PHP published security releases on November 19, 2026: 8.6.0, 8.5.13, 8.4.28, 8.3.37 and 8.2.36. If your site runs PHP (WordPress, Drupal, Joomla, Laravel and most shared hosting do), here is how to find out whether you are concerned and what to do. No panic needed: this is a routine, but large, patch release.

You are concerned if your server runs PHP below these versions:

Older branches (8.1 and below) no longer receive fixes at all.

The PHP 8.5.13 changelog lists fixes in many components: Core, CGI, Date, DOM, FPM, Phar, Session, SOAP, Standard and URI. Several are memory-safety bugs. One that got attention is CVE-2026-129289, a request smuggling issue in PHP-FPM caused by an unread request body. According to Patchstack, the exposed setup is nginx with fastcgi_keep_conn on in front of PHP-FPM. If you do not use that setting, that specific issue is much less relevant to you, but you should still update.

In the reports I checked, these PHP CVEs were not described as exploited in the wild. That can change, so check again rather than rely on this article.

These are PHP flaws, not WordPress core flaws. Updating WordPress does not fix them; updating PHP does.

An outside check only sees what your server shows publicly. Many servers hide their PHP version, which is good practice, so no external tool can promise your patch level. The reliable answer is php -v on your own server.

What you can check from the outside, for free and without an account, is the rest of the picture: the AttackScout public audit runs up to 112 read-only checks on your domain (email authentication, certificate, security headers, end-of-life software signals) and gives an A to F grade. It is a good way to spot other things that tend to be neglected at the same time as a PHP update.

Vibe coding security: a checklist before real users sign up

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 26 • Originally published at pantheontable.com • Fully Autonomous

Originally published at pantheontable.com/blog/vibe-coding-security-checklist

Deleting a leaked API key from your code does not make it safe. Copies stay in your Git history.

If strangers are now typing their details into an app you built by describing it, this checklist is for you. It takes the risks in the order that hurts users most, with what Andrej Karpathy, Pieter Levels, Patrick McKenzie, Jeff Atwood, Arvid Kahl, Simon Willison and Joel Spolsky said about each one, every record linked to its source.

Before you invite more people, close the risks that would hurt them first. Take every API key out of the code and revoke any that leaked, confirm payments on your server and leave card data with the processor, cap what one user can trigger, and prove you can restore a backup. Then have a fresh reviewer audit the whole app, because vibe coding security comes down to the checks the AI did not run for you.

You built the app by describing what you wanted, and now strangers are typing their details into it. Being scared of a leak at this point is reasonable, not paranoid. GitHub's own description of vibe coding separates the quick prototype, where little of the result gets read or tested, from the human-led kind, where you review, test and refine. Real users move you into the second kind, whether you planned it or not.

Checked fact: It separates full vibe coding (little reading or testing of the result, suited to quick experiments and prototypes) from human-led vibe coding (you review, test and refine).

GitHub, resources article 'What is vibe coding?', checked 2026-11-18

Checked fact: Risks it names when checks are skipped: hardcoded credentials, insecure endpoints, unvalidated input, messy structure and technical debt.

GitHub, resources article 'What is vibe coding?', checked 2026-11-18

"Ultimately, vibe coding full web apps today is kind of messy and not a good idea for anything of actual importance. But there are clear hints of greatness and I think the industry just needs a bit of time"

Andrej Karpathy, Vibe coding MenuGen (2025-04-27)

Karpathy wrote this in his essay on vibe coding MenuGen. The hints of greatness are real, and so is the mess you inherit once the thing matters to someone.

Read that list of risks as your checklist. The sections below take them in the order that hurts users most: keys, payments, abuse, AI features, data you cannot get back, and finally a review of the whole app.

An API key in your code works like a password for anyone who can see the code, and the bill or the breach lands on you. When you ask an AI tool to make a demo work, pasting the key straight into the file is the shortest path, so assume it may have happened and look. Your job is to move every key out of the code and keep the file that holds them out of every upload.

Checked fact: Anthropic's guidance: never put an API key directly in your code or configuration files, and add .env files to your ignore file so they are not uploaded.

Anthropic, Claude Help Center 'API key best practices'; GitHub Docs, 'About secret scanning'; Anthropic, Claude Code best practices, checked 2026-11-18

Checked fact: GitHub secret scanning looks for hardcoded API keys, passwords and tokens across the whole Git history of a repository, and public repositories get it automatically for free.

Anthropic, Claude Help Center 'API key best practices'; GitHub Docs, 'About secret scanning'; Anthropic, Claude Code best practices, checked 2026-11-18

Checked fact: In Claude Code a permission deny rule such as Read(./.env) stops the tool from reading the secrets file at all.

Anthropic, Claude Help Center 'API key best practices'; GitHub Docs, 'About secret scanning'; Anthropic, Claude Code best practices, checked 2026-11-18

Checked fact: A public repository is accessible to everyone on the internet; a private one only to its owner and people given access.

GitHub Docs and GitHub pricing page, checked 2026-11-18

Deleting the key from your code does not make it safe, because copies stay in the Git history. The order that works is revoke first, clean up later: kill the old key at the provider and issue a new one. GitHub's docs say that scrubbing a secret out of the history is slow and often unnecessary once the credential has been revoked.

Checked fact: If a key is compromised, delete it immediately in the Console; when a Claude API key appears in a public GitHub repository, GitHub notifies Anthropic and the key is deactivated automatically.

Anthropic, Claude Help Center 'API key best practices'; GitHub Docs, 'About secret scanning'; Anthropic, Claude Code best practices, checked 2026-11-18

Principle: Audit Outbound Communication. Regularly monitor which applications are sending data from your machine to identify and remove suspicious software.

Andrej Karpathy, Digital hygiene (2025-03-17)

Karpathy's advice is to watch which programs send data off your machine and remove the suspicious ones. After a leak, turn the same habit on your app and your laptop.

Payments are where a vibe-coded shortcut costs real money. In the payment documentation we checked, the browser only starts the payment and your server finishes it with a key the browser never sees. A checkout that works in test mode can still skip the server step, so read that part of the code yourself or ask for it to be shown to you.

Checked fact: Flow of the widget: your page loads it with a client key and shows the payment methods, the customer pays, the browser returns to your success address, and your server confirms the payment with the secret key.

Toss Payments, developer center documentation (Korean), checked 2026-11-18

Checked fact: On return, your server should check that the amount matches the amount it saved before confirming.

Toss Payments, developer center documentation (Korean), checked 2026-11-18

"You never see, touch or store any of your customer's credit card data (only a token representing them) and that's how it should be because I wouldn't trust myself to know enough about security to store credit card data anyway."

Pieter Levels, Stripe Launches Beta In The Netherlands

Levels says this about a processor that hands back a token in place of the card. Pick that kind of setup and the most sensitive data never reaches your database.

Handing card data to a processor does not end the payment problem. A small shop with a cheap product and a fast checkout is exactly where stolen cards get tried, and the chargebacks come back to you.

What happened: Fights card testers abusing his $29.95 software. Before Stripe existed, McKenzie spent weeks fighting a gang of criminals who ran $29.95 purchases of his software to test stolen cards before draining victims' bank accounts. With little recourse as an entrepreneur, he eventually spent dozens of hours writing code to outsmart their scripts.

Lesson: A small online seller can become a fraud testing ground, so plan your own fraud detection rather than assume the payment network will protect you.

Patrick McKenzie, Credit Cards As A Legacy System (2022-12-02)

The processor keeps cards off your server; McKenzie's lesson is that it does not keep fraud off your store, so plan some detection of your own.

Principle: Accept non-zero fraud for conversion. McKenzie holds that merchants should trade off conversion rate against fraud rate rather than minimize fraud, since every anti-fraud step also reduces the customers a business can sell to.

Patrick McKenzie, The optimal amount of fraud is non-zero (2022-09-02)

This is the counterweight. Every check you add also turns away some honest buyers, so choose the few that stop the attacks you actually see.

So treat fraud control as a dial, not a wall. Start cheap: limit how many payment attempts one account or address can make in a short time, watch for bursts of small failed charges, and look at failed payments often during your first weeks. Tighten only where you see abuse, and watch your sign-ups when you do.

Principle: Design assuming users will be evil. Atwood holds that social software must be designed from day one assuming some users will be malicious, because naive designs like trackbacks eventually make the whole community suffer.

Jeff Atwood, The Day The Trackbacks Died (2025-06-04)

Trackbacks are his example of a feature built for good-faith users that the whole community paid for once others abused it.

What happened (2024): The Podscan Email Crisis. A single user set up alerts for extremely common words, triggering thousands of emails daily. This caused the email provider to block Arvid's entire domain, stopping even critical login and password reset emails.

Lesson: One user's unintentional actions can threaten the reputation and functionality of your entire business if safeguards aren't in place.

Arvid Kahl, From Overload To Opportunity (2024-10-18)

Kahl's user meant no harm, which is the point: you need limits for honest users too. The damage reached the login and password reset emails every other user depended on.

Podcast summary: Episode 405 | Minimum Viable Security, Moving on from AuditShark, and More Listener Questions. The episode on minimum viable security makes the same case for doing just enough, starting with the basics, and for throttling and isolating a public API. (the episode's official page)

Principle: Never mix untrusted text with tools. Willison holds that an LLM application must never combine untrusted input such as emails or scraped pages with access to tools or private data, because any attacker text can take control of the output.

Simon Willison, Imitation Intelligence, my keynote for PyCon US 2024

If your app reads emails, web pages or user uploads and the same model can also call tools or see private data, text written by an attacker can steer what it does.

If your app puts a model inside the product, such as a chatbot that searches user records or an assistant that sends messages, check each one against this rule. Where the model reads text you did not write, take away its tools and private data, or split the job so the part that reads the text cannot act. Test it yourself by pasting instructions into a message or a page the model will read, and see whether it obeys them.

"Don't rely on your host or anyone else to back up your important data. Do it yourself. If you aren't personally responsible for your own backups, they are effectively not happening."

Jeff Atwood, International Backup Awareness Day (2025-09-11)

A host's backup is a promise you have not checked. A copy you control is the one you can count on.

"The minimum bar for a reliable service is not that you have done a backup, but that you have done a restore ."

Joel Spolsky, Lets Stop Talking About Backups (2009-12-14)

A backup you have never restored from is a guess. Do one restore before you invite more users and see what comes back.

Principle: Run an AI security review before launch. Willison had a frontier AI model run a security evaluation of Datasette Apps before launch, and it found a real data exfiltration attack in his CSP allow-list design.

Simon Willison, Datasette Apps: Host custom HTML applications inside Datasette

The review caught a real attack that could pull data out through his CSP allow-list design, which is why it is worth running even when you believe the app is fine.

"Luckily for us I think the attack and defense capabilities of AI models seems to somewhat match? So as long as we keep typing 'do a security audit' regularly into our clanker we can keep it safe"

Pieter Levels, Claude Code Security Audit Every Vps

Where Willison describes one review before launch, Levels treats the audit as a habit to repeat. You need both: a gate before you open the doors and a routine after.

Checked fact: A review by a fresh agent that never saw the conversation (the /code-review command in Claude Code) is not swayed by the reasoning that produced the code.

Anthropic, Claude Code best practices; GitHub Docs, Copilot best practices; Lovable and Replit documentation, checked 2026-11-18

What should I check first in an app built with AI?

Start with keys: make sure no API key sits in your code or in a public repository, and revoke any that did. Then check that payments are confirmed on your server, that one user cannot trigger unlimited emails or charges, and that you can restore a backup.

Revoke the key at the provider and create a new one before anything else. Removing it from the Git history is slow and often unnecessary once the old key no longer works, and GitHub secret scanning can check the whole history for others.

Is code written by AI less secure than code written by hand?

The risks are the familiar ones, such as hardcoded credentials, insecure endpoints and unvalidated input, but they are easier to miss when you did not write the code. Review and test the result yourself, and ask a fresh reviewer for evidence, not just a verdict.

Should my repository be public or private?

A public repository can be seen by everyone on the internet, so make it private unless it is meant to be open. Never upload keys either way.

Do I need to store credit card numbers to take payments?

No. A payment processor can hold the card and give you a token, and your server confirms the payment with a key the browser never sees, after checking that the amount matches.

If you want to work out which of these matters most for your own app, bring a question like "Here is what my app does, what it stores and which services it calls. Which of these checks matters most for me before the next wave of sign-ups?" to The Pantheon Table. Your business and investing questions are answered there with the real words of world-class masters: https://pantheontable.com/blog/vibe-coding-security-checklist?utm_source=devto&utm_medium=social&utm_campaign=blog

The masters' words are quoted from public records, each with its source. None of them wrote, reviewed or endorses this guide.

Why Users Still Get Stuck With $500 in Stablecoins and $0 in Gas (Solana & TRON Fee Abstraction)

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you build payment tools or checkout flows around stablecoins, you eventually run into the same support ticket: a user receives $200 in USDT or USDC into a self-custody wallet, tries to send $50 somewhere else, and hits a wall because their wallet holds $0.00 of the chain's native gas token.

From a protocol perspective, requiring native SOL or TRX prevents spam. From a product perspective, asking a normal user to go register on an exchange, buy $2 of TRX or SOL, and withdraw it just to move money they already own feels broken.

Over the past few days I dug into the mechanics of how multi-chain wallets like TokenPocket handle this at the wallet layer across two of the busiest retail stablecoin rails: Solana (SPL) and TRON (TRC20). I also put together a short 6-part thread with notes on X here: https://x.com/TuTranHoang_IT/status/2125527942562476313

Here is what actually happens under the hood, along with the real edge cases developers and power users should watch out for.

Base transaction fees on Solana are tiny (fractions of a cent), plus rent-exempt minimums when initializing a new Associated Token Account (ATA). Even a tiny fee blocks a fresh wallet with zero SOL.

In TokenPocket (Android v2.9.0+ and iOS v2.11.0+), the wallet introduces a Daily Free Transfer quota on Solana:

Why cap it at 3 per day and exclude native SOL? Because subsidizing standard SPL token transfers covers 95% of normal peer-to-peer stablecoin usage while preventing automated bots from draining paymaster relayers with high-frequency loops.

TRON is a tougher engineering problem because TRC20 USDT transfers are smart contract executions that consume two separate resources: Bandwidth (byte size) and Energy (TVM execution). When an account has no staked TRX, the network burns TRX directly, which costs noticeably more than a Solana transfer.

Instead of forcing users to swap for TRX first, TokenPocket stacks four separate mechanisms on TRON:

If you are documenting or supporting TRON stablecoin payouts for users, two details trip people up constantly:

I build backend data pipelines, multi-source Python scrapers, and real-time web dashboards. You can test some of my recent interactive prototypes (including a live multi-source business contact scraper & deduplicator) at tranhoangtu-it.github.io/demos.

I built an AI career companion to solve my own job hunting frustrations 🚀

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Job hunting today feels like a full-time job in itself. Between hopping across dozens of company career pages, digging through outdated listings, and scrambling to prepare for interviews, the process can get overwhelming really quickly.

While looking for tech roles myself, I realized how fragmented this workflow was. I wanted a way to keep my job search and interview prep in one place without having 30 tabs constantly open.

So, I built JOBLES (jobles.tech) – a free AI-powered career companion to bring some order to this chaos.

Here’s a quick breakdown of what I tried to solve:

Usually, the freshest and most authentic roles are on the company's own site, but checking them manually is exhausting. JOBLES aggregates open roles directly from company career pages so you can catch genuine listings early.

To help you cut through the noise, you can describe what you're looking for, and JOBLES will help find roles that actually align with your skillset. No more browsing through endlessly generic, outdated boards.

Finding the role is only half the battle. Once you get a bite, you can use JOBLES's integrated AI interview prep tool to practice responses and build confidence for the real thing.

Since I wanted to move fast and keep it lightweight, I built the app using FastAPI (for a robust, speedy backend) paired with an AI/LLM orchestration layer, and designed a clean frontend using HTML, CSS, and JavaScript.

I’m actively working on this side project and need honest feedback from developers, job seekers, and designers.

What features should I add next? What feels clunky? Please roast my project or share your thoughts in the comments below! 👇

‘South Park’ Celebrates ‘GTA VI’ But Outages, AI Data Centers & Zombie Kenny Stand In The Way In Season 29 Finale

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As the long wait for Grand Theft Auto VI finally ends, South Park‘s celebration was cut short on this week’s episode.

In the finale “City Endings,” outages hit the town (currently rebranded as South America for Season 29) thanks to the AI data centers of Billionaire Weenietown that moved into Butters’ backyard after Mayor McDaniels cut a deal to bring them to town.

A mob visits the mayor, who instead of taking responsibility, leads them to believe the outages are “definitely cyber attacks by the Chinese.” That prompts the mob to stake out Mr. Kim’s City Parlor, where they each end up getting a massage with a “city ending.” Watch on Deadline

Meanwhile, Kyle, Stan and Cartman are visited by the ghost of Kenny, who previously died when a bomb strapped to his e-bike exploded.

The trio gives Butters a visit after remembering his connectivity issues while trying to watch Dr. Pimple Popper videos. It turns out, Butters is no longer affected by the outages as he’s since been getting some kickbacks from Billionaire Weenietown, including a massive GTA VI setup.

In the middle of Butters’ gameplay, zombie Kenny appears on the screen, breaking out like Samara in The Ring to deliver a message to his friends.

The episode culminates with the kids confronting the cops and the mayor, only to find out they were all complicit in Kenny’s death when they allowed the billionaires to come to South Park.

Ultimately, Mayor McDaniels takes responsibility for the billionaires ruining the town but sends Mr. Kim to space on WeenieX in order to appease the town. The show ends with “America” falling off the South Park sign after they rolled over to change the town’s name for Trump.

South Park‘s finale wrapped up its Billionaire Weenietown storyline with some more topical jabs, following the massively unprecedented release of GTA VI, as well as the ongoing nationwide outages, which the government has chalked up to many networks changing over to AI systems.

N4 Studio: Senior Webflow Project Manager (Contract)

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We’re looking for a contractSenior Project Manager to own the end-to-end delivery of website and digital projects at N4 Studio, from initial scoping through to launch.

You’ll be responsible for keeping projects moving across design, development and content, making sure timelines, budgets and scope stay on track while keeping both clients and internal teams clear, confident and aligned.

This is a senior delivery role. You’ll need to be comfortable managing multiple projects at once, spotting risks before they become problems, navigating changing priorities and making sure the right people have what they need to deliver great work.

We build exclusively in Webflow, so strong Webflow (or similar) knowledge is important. You don’t need to be a developer, but you do need to understand the platform and how Webflow websites are designed and built so you can successfully scope, plan and manage projects through delivery.

N4 Studio is a global digital agency of around 60 people, specialising in Webflow design, development, and growth. We hold the distinction of being Webflow’s number one agency partner in the world, a position that gives our team direct access to Webflow’s product roadmap, early feature previews, and close collaboration with their global team.

We operate fully remote, with clients and teammates across New Zealand, Australia, North America, the UK and EU. Our culture is built for international collaboration: clear communication, shared ownership, and a genuine respect for people doing great work regardless of where they are located.

This is an opportunity to join a fast-growing global agency and take real ownership of how complex digital projects are delivered, working closely with our clients and teams across design, development and Client Success.

Experienced managing website and digital projects from scoping through to launch

Experienced managing external contractors or freelancers where required

Highly organised, proactive and comfortable taking ownership without needing constant direction

A clear communicator who can keep clients, internal teams and senior stakeholders aligned

Full professional English proficiency required; resume must be submitted in English

Leading website and digital projects from kickoff through to launch

Translating client briefs into clear project plans, timelines, scopes and budgets

Coordinating design, development and content workstreams across multiple projects

Bella Hadid Shares Multiple Health Diagnoses That "Deeply” Impact Her

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bella Hadid detailed how chronic illnesses, including POTS, cause pressure in her career as a model following in the footsteps of her older sister Gigi, as well as how she copes amid the challenges.

Bella Hadid is sharing new revelations about her health.

The 30-year-old explained how managing several medical conditions in addition to Lyme disease—which her mom, Yolanda Hadid, first shared her daughter had in 2015—impacts her, explaining, "My body just can’t connect with my brain."

"I have POTS, and I have a lot of PMDD, endometriosis," Bella told Harper's Bazaar in an interview published Nov. 23. "Plus my Lyme and things that affect my body very deeply."

While premenstrual dysphoric disorder and endometriosis are illnesses related to the female reproductive system that cause painful and exhausting menstrual cycles, according to the Cleveland Clinic, postural orthostatic tachycardia is a syndrome that causes a person's heart to beat faster when they change the position of their body.

And in speaking about her diagnoses, the Beauty actress was brought back to a time when a frightening flare-up threatened to keep her off the runway.

"Right before I walked out on the runway for my opening look, I went to go stretch and my whole clavicle completely—," she recounted. "I don’t even know what it was. My lungs almost collapsed, and I couldn’t breathe."

And with her following the family business in fashion along with Yolanda's other two kids, Gigi Hadid, 31, and Anwar Hadid, 27, Bella admitted the painful interruptions shake her confidence to perform.

"My body goes through these different motions of feeling really good and then all of a sudden crashing on me at the worst moments," she continued. "Because I know how to walk. One thing I can do is I can strut. But it’s a lot of pressure; we all have our own insecurities."

With that in mind, the Victoria's Secret Angel has to remind herself, "maybe my body can’t handle all that I have going on," which ultimately results in the freedom to decide, "'No, we can’t do that today.'"

Earlier this year, the model—who recently went public with her relationship with boyfriend Nathan Lacy—explained how her health struggles can feel isolating.

"Sometimes it feels like unless you've lived something like this, or loved someone who has," Bella captioned a June 26 Instagram Story, "it's impossible to understand."

Read on for more stars who have walked the world through their health challenges.

The Blink-182 drummer stopped smoking weed after being diagnosed with "precancer of the esophagus."

"I loved blunts and I would smoke too much," he shared on a November 2026 episode of the Big Bro with Kid Cudi podcast, recalling how his doctor said it was a "serious case. "He’s like, ‘This is your warning shot. Don’t f--l around. You’ve got to stop smoking."

The Jessie alum opened up about her "hellish" migraines, sharing how they've worsened after suffering five concussions over the course of her life.

"The brain injuries make things significantly worse," Ryan shared during a September 2026 episode of Mythical Kitchen’s Last Meals. "I had a pretty crazy, life-altering brain injury a few years ago, and I was out of commission for six months."

While Ryan described her first four concussions as "incidents," she said her fifth one was trauma caused by "sustained shaking over a minute of time."

Now, the former Disney Channel star needs to close her eyes to "think better."

"Quite often the world is really loud. It’s really too bright. There’s a lot going on," she explained. "The visual data that I'm taking in can be so distracting and so loud. Then, with the migraines—which are significantly worsened by that—it sort of is too much."

Brianna "Chickenfry" LaPaglia's Ovarian Cyst

In May 2026, the BFFs podcaster underwent emergency surgery after doctors discovered a large ovarian cyst that had been twisting her organs and causing internal bleeding.

"There was a half a liter of blood in my pelvic floor," Brianna shared in a TikTok video after the operation. "I have five incisions all over my stomach. They went in through my belly button with a camera so they could find all the blood."

The Total Bellas alum revealed that one of her breast implants shifted after an elbow slam from fellow WWE star Piper Niven during a July 2025 wrestling match.

"It's OK," she told sister Bella Garcia at the time. "We're going to get it fixed one day. I told the doctor, I go, 'Newly divorced, so the minute the boots are hung up, I'm going to come back to you and we're going to make these girls look real good, because these girls are going to get some action in the future and that can't look like that.'"

In October 2026, the former The Bachelorette star detailed how she cracked a vertebra in her spine after setting her vibration plate—a motorized platform that rapidly moves back and forth to promote muscle activation and circulation—to "level 30, supersonic speed."

"Seemingly, subtly, somewhere on the way to 30, maybe 10 or 20, my vertebrae turn crooked and rub against each other," Windey shared in a Substack essaye aptly titled "Broke back, no mountain."

"It was too fast vibrations I guess, and too many vibrations maybe, and too much pressure to shake," she continued. "And it broke. Broke right off, like your ex-boyfriend’s bumper. Well, technically, it cracked."

The injury left her with what she described as "insatiable" paresthesia—a medical term for a tingling, pins-and-needles sensation.

"This is the greatest sense of condemnation; of persecution, I’ll ever feel," she added. "And I do wish it on my worst enemy If only they hadn’t wished it on me first."

The 12 Years a Slave star was diagnosed with uterine fibroids—non-cancerous growths that develop in or around the uterus—in 2014, the same year she won an Oscar for performance in the movie.

However, it wasn't until 2025 when she disclosed her diagnosis, writing on Instagram that she had been "suffering in silence" for more than a decade.

"When we reach puberty, we're taught periods mean pain, and that pain is simply part of being a woman," she shared. "We're struggling alone with something that affects us most."

The Beverly Hills, 90210 alum revealed in April 2025 that he was recovering from surgery after his appendix nearly burst.

“Last week, I started feeling some pain in my stomach,” Brian said in an Instagram video at the time. “I ended up going to the emergency room, and I had a perforated appendix. Not quite burst, but just before.”

He added, “I'm on the road to recovery. It's not an easy process. This is my first major surgery.”

In July 2025, the Daisy Jones & The Six star shared that she had been hospitalized due to a hernia—a gap in the muscular wall that's commonly found in the abdomen or groin area—caused by wearing tight pants.

"'Suki you never tweet anymore,'" she wrote on X, formerly known as Twitter. "Have you ever considered I wore pants so tight 6 months ago it caused a hernia & I’ve been too scared to tell you?"

Savannah DeMelo's Mid-Game "Medical Emergency" Mid-Game

The Racing Louisville midfielder collapsed just a few minutes into her soccer team's September 2025 match against Seattle Reign at Lumen Field in Seatle.

The United States Women’s National Team player could be seen slowly sitting down on the field with a teammate by her side, per Reuters, as the referee called for trainers to check her out. She collapsed seconds later.

“Savannah received immediate on-field care from medical personnel and was transported to a local hospital for further evaluation,” the National Women’s Soccer League wrote on X later that night. “She is stable and alert."

Adam Devine's Long-Term Injury From Childhood Accident

Adam Devine shared that injuries he sustained after getting hit by a cement truck when he was 11 years old are still affecting him to this day.

“It’s been a nightmare,” Devine said in a 2025 episode of the In Depth With Graham Bensinger podcast. “I have spasms all over. For a while, [the doctors] told me I was dying—literally, within this last year.”

He noted that pain comes and goes, adding, "My body has all these things that are a little wonky and a little wrong with it."

The Bachelor Nation alum shared that he went to the hospital “crawling on all fours” due to overwhelming back pain that was aggravated during an RV trip with a friend.

“Right now, knees to my toes, it’s, like, still tingly, which is crazy,” he said on a March 2025 episode of his Trading Secrets podcast. “And to put in perspective, I don’t know, man, like not to sound douchey, but like two, three weeks ago, maybe a month ago, I was squatting probably like 295 pounds. And right now, I can’t do a squat on my foot. I can’t do a one legged squat, I fall. It’s like I had a stroke or something.”

But with the help of a back specialist and some steroids, he added, “We’re on the mend.”

Real Housewives of Orange County’s Gina Kirschenheiter shared her ex-husband Matt Kirschenheiter suffered a heart attack in March 2025, but thankfully survived the ordeal.

The 30 Rock star suffered a health scare at a New York Knicks basketball game in March 2025, leading to him being wheeled out of Madison Square Garden.

However, the comedian shared an update on social media the following day, noting it was a case of food poisoning and that he was on the mend.

The Even Stevens alum shared in February 2025 that she was shot in the face while on a trip to shoot clay pigeons to celebrate husband Brendan Rooney's birthday.

"There was another party with us and they unsafely fired in the wrong direction and shot me in the face," she wrote on Instagram. "@thebrendanrooney immediately sprung into action, assessed me, and rushed me to the hospital. I was hit in 5 places, one was less than an inch from hitting me directly in my right eye."

The Kim Possible alum continued, "Unfortunately a fragment got lodged behind my eye and it is too risky to remove surgically at this time. Doctors will continue to monitor me (I can see normally at the moment)."

The actress said she was grateful to be alive. "I love my daughters, husband, family, and friends so much," she said. "I saw my life flash before my eyes and I’m telling you, hug the people around you every chance you can. Life can change in an instant."

While the internet isn't always a kind place, Amy Schumer is happy that, in this case, it helped her get answers."The internet really came for me after doing a bunch of press, and I was like, 'OK everybody, relax,'" Amy recalled of her "puffier" face on a January 2025 episode of Call Her Daddy. "But then doctors were chiming in in the comments and they were like, 'No no, we think something’s really up. Your face looks so crazy that we think something’s up. And I’m like, ‘Wait, I’m getting trolled by doctors?’"After these doctors said they thought she had Cushing syndrome and that it may be caused by spiking coritsol levels or steroid injections, the Life & Beth creator thought, "Wait, I have been getting steroid injections in my scars.""I had a breast reduction, a C-section, whatever, and so I was getting these steroid injections," Amy said. "So it gave me this thing called Cushing syndrome, which I wouldn’t have known if the internet hadn’t come for me so hard."Toda, Amy is just relieved she's OK. As she shared in a February 2024 News Not Noise newsletter, "Finding out I have the kind of Cushing that will just work itself out and I'm healthy was the greatest news imaginable."

Casey Fitzgerald's Neck Injury From Hockey Skate Blade

The AHL player was cut by a fellow player's skate during a game in December 2024 and received 25 stitches. His father, New Jersey Devils general manager Tom Fitzgerald later told ESPN after his son was fully recovered, "We're very lucky. I don't wish that on any parent."

Hailey Bieber had the "scariest moment" of her life when she was having breakfast with husband Justin Bieber and started experiencing stroke-like symptoms in March 2022."Justin was like, 'Are you OK?'" the Rhode skincare mogul shared a month later on YouTube, "and I just didn't respond because I wasn't sure. And then he asked me again and when I went to respond, I couldn't speak. The right side of my face started drooping. I couldn't get a sentence out."While Hailey said the facial drooping stopped and her speech came back, she went to the hospital to make sure she was OK."They did some scans and they were able to see that I had suffered a small blood clot to my brain," the model added, "which they labeled and categorized as something called a TIA [Transient Ischemic Attack]."Later, Hailey went to the University of California, Los Angeles, where she found out she had a hole in her heart called a patent foramen ovale (PFO).She explained the blood clot had traveled into her heart, "escaped" through the hole and went to her brain, leading to the TIA.Hailey said she had a successful PFO closure procedure and was now feeling great.

The same year Hailey had her health scare, Justin experienced one of his own.In June 2022, the "Baby" singer shared he was diagnosed with Ramsay Hunt syndrome, a condition that caused temporary paralysis to parts of his face and forced him to cancel the remainder of his Justice World Tour."It is from this virus that attacks the nerve in my ear and my facial nerves and has caused my face to have paralysis," Justin explained in an Instagram video at the time. "As you can see, this eye is not blinking. I can't smile on this side of my face. This nostril will not move. So, there's full paralysis on this side of my face."In fact, he told his followers it had gotten "progressively harder to eat." But after a while, the paralysis went away."He's doing really well," Hailey said on a June 2022 episode of Good Morning America, later adding, "He's feeling a lot better. Obviously, it was just a very scary and random situation to happen, but he's going to be totally OK and I'm just grateful that he's fine."

While Jamie Foxx initially kept details of his April 2023 hospitalization private, he later opened up about what he went through.One day in Atlanta, "I was having such a bad headache, so I asked my boy, I said, ‘Listen, I need an aspirin,’” the Oscar winner said in his 2024 Netflix special What Had Happened Was... “Before I could get the Aspirin, I went out. I don’t remember 20 days.”Jamie said he was initially taken to a doctor who gave him a cortisone shot and then sent him home. However, his concerned sister Deidra Dixon then drove him to a hospital, where they got an answer: He had a brain bleed that led to a stroke.Twenty days after undergoing an operation, the Django Unchained woke up May 4 in a wheelchair and couldn’t walk. He then went to Chicago for rehabilitation and therapy."All I can tell you is that I appreciate every prayer,” he said, “because I needed every prayer.”

Tracey Yukich's Medical Emergency on The Biggest Loser

The reality star revealed that she "died" while running a mile during the first challenge of the NBC competition series.

“I don't remember a lot,” she explained on Fit for TV: The Reality of The Biggest Loser. “I remember hearing the helicopter. I just felt like I was floating. And then my grandpa was there. And then I saw darkness. But then I saw light. So I knew, I knew I died that day.”

“My organs were literally shutting down,” she continued. “I didn't realize that I had rhabdomyolysis. And rhabdomyolysis is your body's way of saying, ‘I'm going to shut down on you.’”

Emilia Clarke filmed battle scenes for Game of Thrones, but in 2019, she published an essay in The New Yorker titled "A Battle for My Life."Having a bad headache at the gym, "I reached the toilet, sank to my knees, and proceeded to be violently, voluminously ill," the actress wrote. "Meanwhile, the pain—shooting, stabbing, constricting pain—was getting worse. At some level, I knew what was happening: my brain was damaged."She was taken to the hospital for a brain scan."The diagnosis was quick and ominous: a subarachnoid hemorrhage (SAH), a life-threatening type of stroke, caused by bleeding into the space surrounding the brain," the Emmy nominee added. "I’d had an aneurysm, an arterial rupture."Emilia had immediate surgery to seal the aneurysm, calling the pain "unbearable." While she was recovering, she continued, she experienced aphasia and was "muttering nonsense."A week later, "the aphasia passed," Emilia added, and she left the hospital a month after being admitted. At a 2013 brain scan, she learned a growth "doubled in size" and that she needed surgery again."When they woke me, I was screaming in pain," she wrote. "The procedure had failed. I had a massive bleed and the doctors made it plain that my chances of surviving were precarious if they didn’t operate again. This time they needed to access my brain in the old-fashioned way—through my skull."Thankfully, Emilia shared, she's now "at a hundred per cent."

Snoop Dogg's daughter Cori Broadus is grateful for her family.Because after she suffered a "severe stroke" in January 2024, her loved ones rushed to be by her side."I texted them that I just had a stroke and sent them a picture," she told E! News correspondent Will Marfuggi in December 2024. "Everybody just came to my rescue."Because of other medical concerns, Cori ended up staying in the hospital longer than she initially expected."I could have went home after my stroke, but my lupus wasn't doing so well," the Snoop's Fatherhood: Cori and Wayne's Story star continued. "My kidneys were failing. It was a lot going on."Today, Cori is on the mend."I'm doing great," she added. "I just had a little bit of motor skills that I had to work back on. But other than that, I was fine."

Shailene Woodley’s early 20s were not an easy time health-wise.“It got to the point where I was losing my hearing. I couldn’t walk for longer than five minutes at a time without having to lay down for hours, and hours and sleep. Everything I ate hurt my stomach,” the actress said on a September 2024 episode of the SHE MD Podcast. “It was this conflation of issues and diagnoses and different doctors telling me different things.”So Shailene—who chose to keep the exact condition private—set out to find answers. “I come from a very holistic background and study herbalism,” she added. “So I was very keen on, ‘I’m going to work with real MDs, [and] I’m also going to work with independent kind of healers.’ Just trying to search for some sense of comfort in my own skin.”It was a long journey—one that lasted for 10 years.“Throughout that decade, a lot of other things came from feeling so much discomfort physically,” the Big Little Lies star noted, “which was, ‘My gosh, if everything I eat hurts my stomach, I’m now suddenly afraid of food.’ And then going into the kind of mental f--ckery that can happen with that of body dysmorphia and confusion about identity and feeling safe in my own capsule, in my own skin, what that meant, and what that should be.”After tending to both her physical and mental health, Shailene is feeling much better.“It was a journey that ultimately physically resolved itself, and I am very healthy,” she shared. “I’m so happy to be able to say that."

Google Adds Pageburst & 11 Other Existing Bots To Its Crawler Docs via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

Google's crawler docs now list pageburst, which renders pages for Google Ads and ignores robots.txt, plus more existing user agents to check in server logs.

Google identified pageburst as a user agent that renders pages to measure Google Ads quality and landing page safety and ignores robots.txt rules. Its two strings look like a browser’s and do not contain “Googlebot”; a bot rule created using “Googlebot” will not capture them.

Pageburst is one of 12 existing user agents Google added to its crawler documentation on Nov. 23, along with a corrected entry for APIs-Google. Google’s crawling documentation changelog explains:

“These crawlers and fetchers are not new; we’re documenting existing high-volume clients that are more likely to show up in server logs and that we’ve received questions about.”

The 12 belong to Google products other than Search, such as Google Ads, Gmail and Google Cloud.

Google’s special-case crawlers page, which lists crawlers used by specific Google products, says pageburst “renders web pages for Google Ads quality, landing-page safety, and page understanding.” The entry adds that “it’s unaffected by crawling preferences.”

The desktop string identifies as Chrome on Linux and the mobile one as Chrome for iOS on an iPad, and both include “compatible; pageburst.”

AdsBot, which checks web page ad quality for Google Ads, has its own robots.txt token. Pageburst’s entry lists no token and says the user agent “ignores robots.txt rules.”

The highlights show what to look for in server logs and where Google states its robots.txt policy.

Google’s documentation says user-agent strings can be faked. Special-case crawlers use the IP addresses defined in Google’s special-crawlers.json; these have a reverse-DNS match of rate-limited-proxy-***-***-***-***.google.com. The pageburst entry does not say what occurs when a website blocks the Google Ads check.

Besides pageburst, each of the five other newly documented special-case entries says the user agent “ignores robots.txt rules.”

Google’s user-triggered fetchers page covers the six newly documented fetchers and says that because a user requested the fetch, these fetchers “generally ignore robots.txt rules.” Three of those (AppEngine-Google, Google-Apps-Script and Google-Cloud-Tasks) send requests for code running within Google’s platform-based products. The remaining three are GoogleImageProxy, which is used to load image content into email applications such as Gmail, along with Google Web Preview and the PageSpeed Insights fetcher, both with strings ending in Chrome-Lighthouse.

Several of the newly listed user agents seem to use standard browser strings. For example, a string from GoogleImageProxy appears similar to Firefox 11 on Windows NT 5.1; the strings for Google Web Preview and PageSpeed Insights, and one of PlayStore-Google’s, were created using Chrome strings. A filter that only identifies “Googlebot” would miss all of these.

Google has moved APIs-Google, which makes requests to the webhook notification systems developers create within Google Cloud and Google Workspace APIs, from the list of special-case crawlers to the list of user-triggered fetchers. APIs-Google’s push requests, the changelog says, “come from the user-triggered fetcher IP ranges and ignore robots.txt rules.” Google added a missing semicolon after “APIs-Google” in its documented string and tells developers to modify any code that was previously using a hardcoded version of the earlier string.

Prior to the Nov. 23 update, Google had included robots.txt as a method to block requests from APIs-Google in its documentation. In an Oct. 21 capture, the robots.txt token was present on the special-case page. On Oct. 30, a capture of the APIs-Google page showed “Use robots.txt” as one of two methods to block requests from APIs-Google. Now the APIs-Google page says that it “ignores robots.txt rules”; for controlling the delivery of notifications, the page points to either the developer who created the notification system or Google Cloud’s abuse form for unexpected requests.

Pageburst renders pages for ads in Google and does not follow robots.txt. Check for “pageburst” in the log entries for your servers and CDNs, verify with Google’s procedures that the IP addresses are in its special-crawler ranges, and see if your pages were delivered to the verified requests, or if a challenge or a 403 was returned.

Roger Montti covered Google’s advice on Nov. 18 that general bot blocking may keep Googlebot from crawling content when moving a CDN; the same advice should be applied to other Google clients that do not have the name Googlebot.

Google’s special-case and user-triggered pages both say their lists aren’t exhaustive and cover the requestors “more likely to show up in log files.” The crawling documentation changelog, which is where Google logs additions like these, has an RSS feed.

Crypto host who once promoted a $50 billion memecoin vision now tells traders to get out

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ansem’s wallet hack exposed security risks at Bullpen and prompted renewed debate over crypto's reputation.

Jonah, co-host of the 1000x podcast, wrote on Nov. 24 to “get out while you still can” after crypto trader Ansem said his wallet had been hacked.

Jonah predicted that staying in crypto would carry a stigma. At 03:07 UTC on Nov. 25, trading app Bullpen said the known impact of its breach was limited to 19 early beta accounts.

At 03:53 UTC, Ansem advised users to withdraw their funds until he could confidently say they were safe.

Ansem’s initial post said he suspected that someone had accessed the Bullpen profile where he had imported the private key for a Solana wallet he used on Pump.fun, and Jonah’s quote-post came in response to that claim.

Bullpen’s first public notice came later Tuesday and identified Ansem as one of the users affected.

In its initial notice, Bullpen gave a preliminary range of 18 to 19 affected accounts, all opened between April and June 2024. The company said it took the affected test service offline.

Its Wednesday update gave a figure of 19 accounts and said it had seen no further movement of the funds at that point.

Bullpen said the attacker took over the older accounts, exposing wallets users had imported into them. For users who had imported a wallet, the access Bullpen described extended beyond the trading app to funds in that wallet.

The private key Ansem said he imported could authorize transfers from the wallet he used on Pump.fun. The company said 99.8% of the funds taken came from one account, which it did not name.

The updates describe efforts to recover the money and leave the recovered amount undisclosed.

In his follow-up, Ansem said two of his Solana wallets were compromised, identifying them as the wallet he imported from Pump.fun and a wallet already on Bullpen.

He attributed the breach to a weakness in older accounts that he said let someone bypass Bullpen’s two-factor login. Ansem also said a separate BitGo wallet requiring multiple signatures, which he said held 49% of the ANSEM token supply, was safe and unmoved.

An ANSEM sell-off unfolded on Nov. 24, driven by a large ANSEM sell-off from a publicly labeled wallet. Bitquery reported about $2.57 million in USDC at one Arbitrum address at 17:00 UTC that day.

Jonah’s comments drew a quote-post from user soby and a “This you?” reply from @CupOJoseph. In another reply, Jonah said he liked Ansem and had helped launch a token a couple of years earlier.

Jonah had published a BODEN roadmap in April 2024. The post imagined the memecoin listing on the New York Stock Exchange at a $50 billion valuation and becoming a reserve currency.

In a separate post early Wednesday, he distinguished between launching a token he considered worthless and selling it, which he said made someone a scammer.

In his own post Tuesday, 1000x co-host Avi Felman wrote that “crypto is fine,” arguing the industry grew stronger through previous downturns.

House Committee advances Strategic Bitcoin Reserve bill with narrow path to passage

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The current 119th Congress is expected to adjourn in the coming weeks, leaving little time for a House floor vote, Senate consideration, and final passage before the session ends.

The House Financial Services Committee has advanced H.R. 8957, the American Reserve Modernization Act of 2026, which would codify a Strategic Bitcoin Reserve and impose a minimum 20-year lock on federally held bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC).

The legislation, introduced on May 21 by Rep. Nick Begich, would allow the Treasury secretary to establish a Strategic Bitcoin Reserve and a separate Digital Asset Stockpile for other cryptocurrencies 180 days after enactment.

Once deposited in the reserve, bitcoin cannot be "sold, swapped, auctioned, encumbered, or otherwise disposed of for any purpose" for at least 20 years. The bill also includes transparency requirements such as proof-of-reserve reporting and directs federal agencies to report existing holdings.

On Sept. 16, the House Financial Services Committee approved it on a party-line vote of 28-21, with support from every Republican representative and opposition from every Democrat. On Nov. 24, the committee officially sent the revised bill to the full House, where it is now eligible for a floor vote.

The bill builds on U.S. President Donald Trump's executive order issued in March 2025 that directed the creation of a bitcoin reserve funded mainly through bitcoin already owned by the government from criminal or civil forfeitures, and a separate digital asset stockpile. The order sparked concern from some lawmakers on bitcoin's riskiness and volatility.

While the bill is now on the Union Calendar as Calendar No. 789, its chances of passing are low. The current 119th Congress is expected to adjourn in the coming weeks, leaving little time for a House floor vote, Senate consideration, and final passage before the session ends.

Polymarket odds show a 4% chance of the U.S. establishing a national bitcoin reserve by the end of this year, down from 60% around a year ago and 34% in May this year.

If the bill fails to pass within this Congress, it would have to be reintroduced in the 120th Congress. The Strategic Bitcoin Reserve created by the March 2025 executive order would remain in place, but a future president could alter or reverse it without congressional approval. Reintroducing and advancing the initiative may face steeper challenges as Democrats are set to control both chambers starting in January.

US announces new tariffs on host of countries including China

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: What we know about Trump's Thanksgiving tariffs

The US has announced a fresh raft of tariffs as President Donald Trump pushes ahead with his signature trade policy.

The new taxes charged on imports to America are set to hit many of the country's biggest trading partners - China, the European Union and 12 other jurisdictions, including Japan, South Korea and Taiwan.

The White House began investigating the trade practices of other countries in March after the US Supreme Court ruled Trump's previously announced Liberation Day tariffs were illegal.

China is facing the highest tariff rate of up to 40%, followed by Vietnam at up to 35%, with others facing either 20% or 25%.

The US Trade Representative (USTR) announced the proposed tariffs together with extensive reports of how each jurisdiction's trade practices purportedly impact American commerce.

The new duties are not expected to take effect until next year and have to go through rounds of public consultation and, potentially, hearings.

Tariffs are taxes levied on imported goods. The amount is typically calculated as a percentage of a good's value and paid to the government by businesses importing products.

Some of these rates will be effectively reduced because of caps on tariffs Trump has agreed to. Individual countries also face different levies.

The proposed tariffs will affect goods from China, the European Union, Singapore, Switzerland, Indonesia, Malaysia, Cambodia, Thailand, Korea, Vietnam, Taiwan, Bangladesh, Japan, and India.

The USTR's Jamieson Greer said the latest tariffs issued would "level the playing field for American workers and chart a new, more sustainable course for the world economy".

He said that many of America's trading partners had "for decades" adopted industrial strategies which included "targeting manufacturing, wage and labor suppression".

They also employ "other market distorting practices that tilt the trade balance in their favour while eroding America's manufacturing base", Greer said.

But economists have warned tariffs can fuel inflation and push prices up for consumers if businesses pass on higher costs, as well as dampen global trade.

Trump has repeatedly threatened or imposed tariffs during his second term, arguing they will boost US manufacturing and create jobs.

Despite a poor result for the Republican party in the midterm elections, the president has continued to press on with his flagship trade policy.

Trump is also seeking to cut the US trade deficit - the gap between the value of goods it buys from other countries and those it sells to them.

Trump's so-called Liberation Day tariffs were struck down by the Supreme Court earlier this year

These new levies are build on a "stronger legal foundation" under US law compared with Trump's April 2025 Liberation Day tariffs, Singapore Management University economics lecturer Chang Pao-Li said.

Trump previously invoked emergency presidential powers to impose tariffs that have since been struck down, while his latest approach relies on the USTR's authority to impose tariffs to address certain foreign trade practices, she said.

The levies are still likely to face legal challenges, but there has been a "historical precedent" for such tariffs remaining in force, Chang said.

Many tariffs imposed under Section 301 - a law that allows the US to probe and retaliate against foreign trade practices - during Trump's first term remained in effect during his successor Joe Biden's administration.

However, Stephen Brown, chief North America economist for Capital Economics, said the latest tariff announcement was "not as alarming as it looks", due to the taxes "essentially replacing" previously announced tariffs made in the summer.

"The increase in the effective tariff rate looks set to be a touch lower than we've been assuming for our forecasts, although the new tariffs will still provide a marginal boost to prices," he said in a note.

The additional tariff burden is likely to be small because a limited number of goods are subject to the new duties, Brown added.

Jurisdictions affected by the new wave of tariffs have voiced their disapproval over the Trump administration's move.

Washington has also proposed a 10% tariff on goods from Brazil, as part of a separate investigation over what it says are "harmful trade practices" undercutting American workers.

Brazilian authorities expressed their "outrage" at the announcement, saying that the move is aimed at pressuring the government to change regulations around issues like digital trade.

The South American nation faces duties on finished aircraft and sugar sold to the US.

"Brazil will not yield to such threats," President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva's office said.

The European Commission rejected the new US tariffs, saying it would investigate the reasons for bringing them in. It pointed out that tariffs are limited to the 15% previously agreed.

The latest tariff rate is "not consistent" with the reality of bilateral economic-trade cooperation between the US and Vietnam, Vietnamese Deputy Prime Minister Bui Thanh Son said.

The Japanese government said the US tariffs were "regrettable".

Trump tariffs on Asia: Which economies were hit hardest?

China's growth accelerates as AI boom helps boost exports

US trading partners have been "waiting for this shoe to drop" and have argued that they are being wrongly accused of producing too much, said the Asia Society Policy Institute's Wendy Cutler, a former senior US trade negotiator.

"Many believe that China, not them, should be the target for US action," she added.

Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson Guo Jiakun described the new tariffs as a form of "political manipulation", describing as "false" the US claim that China produces more than it can consume.

The tariff announcement is likely to increase tensions between the US and China, the world's two biggest economies, who remain locked in a trade dispute and the AI race.

The two countries have also been embroiled in a tit-for-tat tariffs war, which is currently on hold.

Despite last week's meeting between Trump and China's President Xi Jinping, the two sides made no announcement on the trade war truce, which is set to expire on 10 January.

Beijing has remained vague on how it will respond but for now seems "prepared to live with the new rates provided they don't exceed 20%", Cutler said.

Additional reporting by Leehyun Choi in Seoul

Acura Makes Japanese-Market Debut with the U.S.-Built Integra Type S

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Integra Type S is now for sale in Japan, making it the first Acura-branded vehicle to reach the Japanese market.

The 2026 Acura Integra Type S is officially for sale in Japan. That might not seem like big news, considering Honda Motor Company is headquartered in Japan, but the release marks the first Acura-branded vehicle to be exported to the Japanese market. While this is Acura’s first time in Japan, it’s strangely not the Integra’s, which spent a few decades wearing a Honda badge from 1985 to 2006.

Most Acuras are designed and built in the United States and sold throughout North America. The new Integras for sale in Japan are the same as those sold stateside, meaning they’ll be left-hand-drive anomalies in a predominantly right-hand-drive market.

See all results for new 2027 Acura Integra Type S for sale near 20147

That also means they’ll get the same 320-hp turbocharged 2.0-liter inline-four that we’re quite fond of in the United States. The front-wheel-drive sport compact is only offered with a six-speed manual transmission and features a limited-slip differential, Brembo brakes, and 19-inch wheels wrapped in summer tires. The whole package comes together to reach 60 mph in a brisk 5.1 seconds.

Honda builds every Integra Type S at its assembly plant in Marysville, Ohio, and the engines come from Honda’s Anna Engine Plant, also located in Ohio. Honda began exporting vehicles from Ohio to Japan in 1988 and has shipped nearly 300,000 Hondas to Japan since then.

Honda says the Teg’s introduction in Japan marks a strong global demand for an enthusiast-centered performance sedan. If the Type S is well-received in the Japanese market, it’s likely that more Acuras could start making their way to Japan in the future. Pricing and the exact number of Integra Type S models available in Japan haven’t been released yet, but considering they’re identical to the Type S models sold on our shores, they’ll likely come in around $56,000.

NYT Strands hints, answers for November 26, 2026

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Today's NYT Strands hints will be easy if you're a good friend.

Strands, the New York Times' elevated word-search game, requires the player to perform a twist on the classic word search. Words can be made from linked letters — up, down, left, right, or diagonal, but words can also change direction, resulting in quirky shapes and patterns. Every single letter in the grid will be part of an answer. There's always a theme linking every solution, along with the "spangram," a special, word or phrase that sums up that day's theme, and spans the entire grid horizontally or vertically.

By providing an opaque hint and not providing the word list, Strands creates a brain-teasing game that takes a little longer to play than its other games, like Wordle and Connections.

If you're feeling stuck or just don't have 10 or more minutes to figure out today's puzzle, we've got all the NYT Strands hints for today's puzzle you need to progress at your preferred pace.

Today's NYT Strands spangram is horizontal.

Wordle today: Answer, hints for November 26, 2026

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Today's Wordle answer should be easy to solve if you're always on edge.

If you just want to be told today's word, you can jump to the bottom of this article for today's Wordle solution revealed. But if you'd rather solve it yourself, keep reading for some clues, tips, and strategies to assist you.

Originally created by engineer Josh Wardle as a gift for his partner, Wordle rapidly spread to become an international phenomenon, with thousands of people around the globe playing every day. Alternate Wordle versions created by fans also sprang up, including battle royale Squabble, music identification game Heardle, and variations like Dordle and Quordle that make you guess multiple words at once.

Wordle eventually became so popular that it was purchased by the New York Times, and TikTok creators even livestream themselves playing.

Are you also playing NYT Connections? See hints and answers for today's Connections.

The best Wordle starting word is the one that speaks to you. But if you prefer to be strategic in your approach, we have a few ideas to help you pick a word that might help you find the solution faster. One tip is to select a word that includes at least two different vowels, plus some common consonants like S, T, R, or N.

It might feel like Wordle is getting harder, but it actually isn't any more difficult than when it first began. You can turn on Wordle's Hard Mode if you're after more of a challenge, though.

Microdrama Revenue to Hit $20 Billion in 2026, but Profits Outside China Remain Thin, Media Partners Asia Report Finds

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Global microdrama revenue will climb 24% to $20 billion in 2026, but outside China the business has yet to prove it can make reliable money, according to new research from Media Partners Asia.

The Asia-focused research and advisory firm’s second annual study of the sector, “The Microdrama Economy 2026,” projects the market will reach $32 billion by 2031, on annual growth of 9.8%. MPA’s figures measure spending by viewers, advertisers and brands before app stores and payment processors take their cut. Outside China, they count only dedicated microdrama apps and their web storefronts, not general social media feeds. Related Stories Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Designates the Troubadour as a Landmark Venue; Going Forward, L.A. Will Host Hall's Induction Ceremony 'At Least Every Other Year' (EXCLUSIVE) Phil Collins Says He’s a ‘Well Man’ Now and Hints at the ‘Possibility of Doing Some Shows’ Amid Health Battle: 'You Haven't Heard the Last of Me'

China still dominates. The country will generate $15.2 billion this year, or 76% of the global total, after revenue jumped 90% in 2025. MPA expects growth there to cool to 6.7% annually as viewing levels off, taking Chinese revenue to $21 billion by 2031. The firm’s China figure is narrower than the National Radio and Television Administration’s RMB100 billion ($15 billion) estimate for 2025, which also counts producer outlays on AI models and platform investment.

Free, ad-supported viewing overtook paid viewing in China in 2025. Advertising will make up 69% of the country’s microdrama revenue this year and 79% by 2031. ByteDance, which operates Hongguo and Douyin, takes 56% of Chinese revenue, and ByteDance, Tencent and Kuaishou together command 82%.

Dedicated apps outside China will earn $4.87 billion in 2026, a third more than last year, with MPA forecasting $11 billion by 2031 on annual growth of 17.7%. The U.S. remains the biggest single market at $1.71 billion, though that is 2% lower than in 2025. Asia-Pacific excluding China, at $1.44 billion, is set to overtake the U.S. in 2029.

India, at $322 million, now outranks Japan and Korea as the biggest market in Asia after China. It is also the fastest-growing, with revenue more than tripling this year. Local players Kuku TV and Story TV both roughly tripled their takings, and JioStar launched its microdrama service Tadka on JioHotstar in April. India is the only big market where local platforms lead, selling cheap subscriptions that bypass app-store billing. JioStar’s existing streaming audience has reduced its need to pay for new users, and the company is now turning to retention and growing ad revenue.

Revenue is concentrated. ReelShort, DramaBox–GoodShort, Kunlun and NetShort together generate 65% of the total outside China, and each is either owned or financed from China. DramaBox and GoodShort announced a merger on Oct. 31, the first such deal among the sector’s top players, partly so the two would stop outbidding one another for advertising. Fox, Versant and NBCUniversal are among the U.S. media companies that have bought minority stakes in the sector, while India’s and Korea’s biggest local platforms are gearing up to go public.

Some homegrown companies take on the Chinese-owned apps directly, among them Korea’s Vigloo and Fuji TV’s FOD Short in Japan. MPA sees their edge in what the Chinese apps don’t have: built-in audiences, homegrown IP and talent, and a way to bill viewers without payment cards. Japanese and Korean broadcasters, studios and telcos mostly work as producers. Across Southeast Asia, telcos have sold heavily discounted passes to build reach, though MPA said it is unclear whether these bring in new paying customers or simply shift existing ones to a cheaper channel.

TikTok sits on all sides of the market. It sells advertising to the apps, carries their series and competes with them through its free app PineDrama. In Southeast Asia, ByteDance is repeating its China playbook, building audiences with free, ad-funded apps before cashing in. MPA expects TikTok to become a major player in the U.S. and Southeast Asia alike by 2031. YouTube stays open to creators and studios, while Meta mainly serves as the place where apps find new users. Even so, MPA doesn’t expect free platforms to push out the paid apps, which control the IP and the billing relationship with paying customers.

Profits are the sticking point. For each dollar of revenue generated outside China, 56 cents goes to marketing and another 18 cents to app stores and payment processors. In a typical month, just one in 12 users or so pays anything. The two largest groups each bring in more than $1 billion a year, yet DramaBox posted a 3.2% net margin in fiscal 2025. ReelShort lost $12 million in 2025 before swinging to a profit of roughly $8 million in the January-March quarter of 2026.

“Demand for microdrama is established in every major market, but outside China this is still an emerging business, and it has yet to show that it can earn a durable profit,” said Vivek Couto, CEO and executive director of Media Partners Asia. “The leading apps spend more than half of their revenue to acquire viewers, and most of those viewers do not pay. Consolidation has begun, and China has already moved to free, advertising-funded viewing. The companies that endure will be those that retain their viewers, bill them directly and invest in titles worth returning to.”

AI is reshaping supply far faster than demand. By MPA’s count, new titles listed on non-China apps will surge from around 100,000 in 2025 to 440,000 this year, with the AI-generated share climbing from 15% to 75%. Each listing represents a single title in a single language on a single app, so a series that runs in multiple languages or across multiple apps shows up more than once. Producing a title with AI costs roughly 10% of what a Los Angeles shoot would.

Audiences have been slower to switch. Live-action titles hold most of the top 50 spots on five of the six non-China platforms MPA tracks. In China, about nine in 10 titles released in 2026 are AI-made, but they bring in just 20% of the year’s revenue.

“What decides a platform’s economics is how many of its titles earn back the marketing spent on them,” said Myat Pan Phyu, MPA’s lead analyst for microdrama and vertical video. “That favors companies with established IP, stars and audiences of their own, which is why local broadcasters, studios and telecom operators matter to the next phase.”

Payments may shift the math. Apple and Google process 65% of the revenue earned outside China, though legal rulings and fresh legislation in the U.S., Japan and Korea have cleared the way for web payments. Direct billing can lift margins, although the savings depend on each market’s fees and payment route. MPA expects payments made outside the app stores, including through India’s UPI system, to deliver roughly half of the growth outside China in 2027.
